\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 68.9}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-32.99454pt}{48.72984pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -45.65 67.43)} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 68.9}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-4.54178pt}{48.72984pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -6.28 67.43)} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 39.37 68.9}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{23.91098pt}{48.72984pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 33.09 67.43)} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{array}.

(S39)

From here we can construct the resulting
C^{*}
-weak Hopf algebra using the diagramatic approach in Sec.
B
. Similar reconstruction can be found in
[
63
]
. For convenience, we choose the 3-cocycle
\omega
to be normalized, i.e.,
\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})=1
if some of
\{g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}\}
is the unit of the group. Let us construct the
C^{*}
-weak Hopf algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}}
such that
\text{Rep}(\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}})=\text{Rep}(\mathcal{A}_{\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}})=\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}
: The algebra is spanned by basis
e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h)}
, where
g_{1},g_{2},h\in G
and has a dimension of
|G|^{3}
with
|G|
being the order of the group. The multiplication is defined as

e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h_{1})}\cdot e_{g_{3},g_{4}}^{(h_{2})}=\frac{\omega(h_{1},h_{2},g_{3})}{\omega(h_{1},h_{2},g_{4})}\delta_{g_{1},h_{2}g_{3}}\delta_{g_{2},h_{2}g_{4}}e_{g_{3},g_{4}}^{(h_{1}h_{2})},

(S40)

and the comultiplication is defined as

\Delta[e_{g_{1}g_{2}}^{(h)}]=\sum_{g^{\prime}}e^{(h)}_{g_{1}g^{\prime}}\otimes e^{(h)}_{g^{\prime}g_{2}}.

(S41)

The unit, counit, antipode, and *-operation are as follows:

\begin{split}&{\bf 1}=\sum_{g_{1},g_{2}}e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(I)},\quad\epsilon(e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h)})=\delta_{g_{1},g_{2}},\\
&S(e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h)})=\frac{\omega(h^{-1},h,g_{1})}{\omega(h^{-1},h,g_{2})}e_{hg_{2},hg_{1}}^{(h^{-1})},\\
&[e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h)}]^{*}=\frac{\omega(h^{-1},h,g_{2})}{\omega(h^{-1},h,g_{1})}e_{hg_{1},hg_{2}}^{(h^{-1})}.\end{split}

(S42)

The faithful *-representations for the algebra and dual algebra can be chosen as

\begin{split}&\Phi[e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h)}]=\omega^{-1}(h,g_{1},g_{1}^{-1}g_{2})|hg_{1},hg_{2}\rangle\langle g_{1},g_{2}|,\\
&\Psi[\tilde{e}^{(h)}_{g_{1},g_{2}}]=|h,g_{1})(h,g_{2}|,\end{split}

(S43)

where
\tilde{e}^{(h)}_{g_{1},g_{2}}
is the dual of
e^{(h)}_{g_{1},g_{2}}
and the matrix multiplication of
\Phi
is consistent with the algebraic multiplication due to the cocycle condition. We have also used the Dirac notation to represent matrices on basis
|g_{1},g_{2}\rangle
and
|g_{1},g_{2})
for the physical and virtual spaces, respectively. Both
\Phi
and
\Psi
furnish complete decompositions into the
|G|
irreducible representations of
\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}}
, respectively, with each irrep appearing once. The representation
\Psi
contains irreps labeled by
h
, which we denote as
\Psi_{h}
:
\Psi_{h}[\tilde{e}_{g_{1}g_{2}}^{(h^{\prime})}]=\delta_{hh^{\prime}}|h,g_{1})(h,g_{2}|
. Correspondingly, we can obtain
O_{h}^{(L)}
, generated by the tensor
\sum_{h^{\prime},g_{1},g_{2}}\Phi[e_{g_{1},g_{2}}^{(h^{\prime})}]\otimes\Psi_{h}[\tilde{e}^{(h^{\prime})}_{g_{1},g_{2}}]
, satisfying the multiplication rule of group
G
:

O_{h_{1}}^{(L)}O_{h_{2}}^{(L)}=O_{h_{1}h_{2}}^{(L)},

(S44)

and from the formalism developed in the main text, the MPDO fixed-point can be assembled as:

\rho_{\text{RFP},m}^{(L)}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{m}}O^{(L)}(\Pi_{m})\Omega^{\otimes L},

(S45)

where
\Omega=\mathbb{1}_{|G|^{2}}/|G|
since
\text{Rep}\mathcal{A}_{\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}}\cong\text{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}
in our construction and every irrep has quantum dimension
1
. Given any 1D irrep
m
of the group
G
, the explicit form of
\Pi_{m}
is

\Pi_{m}=\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{h\in G}\chi_{m}(h^{-1})P_{h},

(S46)

where
\chi_{m}
is the character of the irrep
m
of the group, and
P_{h}
is the Hermitian projector from
\Psi
onto irrep
\Psi_{h}
.
The resulting MPDO fixed points have a strong MPO symmetry
\{O_{h}^{(L)}\}_{h\in G}
that are anomalous due to nontrivial 3-cocycle and are topologically nontrivial for similar reason as discussed in
[
10
]
.

V
Example of Fibonacci fusion category

Here we give details about the
C^{*}
-weak Hopf algebra from Fibonacci fusion category
\mathcal{C}_{\text{Fib}}
. As discussed in the main text, the reconstructed
C^{*}
-weak Hopf algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbb{C})
. One can choose the basis
e_{\alpha,ij}
where for
\alpha=I
,
i,j\in\{1,2\}
and for
\alpha=\tau
,
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
. The multiplication follows the rules of matrix multiplication,

e_{\alpha,ij}e_{\alpha^{\prime},kl}=\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{jk}e_{\alpha,il}.

(S47)

The comultiplication is defined as follows
[
56
]
:

\begin{split}\Delta(e_{I,11})&:=e_{I,11}\otimes e_{I,11}+e_{\tau,11}\otimes e_{\tau,22},\\
\Delta(e_{I,12})&:=e_{I,12}\otimes e_{I,12}+\zeta^{2}\,e_{\tau,12}\otimes e_{\tau,21}+\zeta e_{\tau,13}\otimes e_{\tau,23},\\
\Delta(e_{I,22})&:=e_{I,22}\otimes e_{I,22}+\zeta^{4}\,e_{\tau,22}\otimes e_{I,11}+\\
&\zeta^{3}\,e_{\tau,23}\otimes e_{\tau,13}+\zeta^{3}\,e_{\tau,32}\otimes e_{\tau,31}+\zeta^{2}\,e_{\tau,33}\otimes e_{\tau,33},\\
\Delta(e_{\tau,11})&:=e_{I,11}\otimes e_{\tau,11}+e_{\tau,11}\otimes e_{I,11}+e_{\tau,11}\otimes e_{\tau,33},\\
\Delta(e_{\tau,12})&:=e_{I,12}\otimes e_{\tau,12}+e_{\tau,12}\otimes e_{I,21}+e_{\tau,13}\otimes e_{\tau,32},\\
\Delta(e_{\tau,13})&:=e_{I,12}\otimes e_{\tau,13}+e_{\tau,13}\otimes e_{\tau,22}+\\
&\zeta\,e_{\tau,12}\otimes e_{\tau,31}-\zeta^{2}\,e_{\tau,13}\otimes e_{\tau,33},\\
\Delta(e_{\tau,22})&:=e_{I,22}\otimes e_{\tau,22}+e_{\tau,22}\otimes e_{I,11}+e_{\tau,33}\otimes e_{\tau,22},\\
\Delta(e_{\tau,23})&:=e_{I,22}\otimes e_{\tau,23}+e_{\tau,23}\otimes e_{\tau,12}+\zeta e_{\tau,32}\otimes e_{\tau,21}-\\
&\zeta^{2}\,e_{\tau,33}\otimes e_{\tau,23},\\
\Delta(e_{\tau,33})&:=e_{I,22}\otimes e_{\tau,33}+e_{\tau,33}\otimes e_{I,22}+\zeta^{2}e_{\tau,22}\otimes e_{\tau,11}-\\
&\zeta^{3}\,e_{\tau,23}\otimes e_{\tau,13}-\zeta^{3}\,e_{\tau,32}\otimes e_{\tau,31}+\zeta^{4}\,e_{\tau,33}\otimes e_{\tau,33},\end{split}

(S48)

with
\zeta=[(\sqrt{5}-1)/2]^{1/2}
. The comultiplication of remaining elements can be obtained from the *-operation which is antilinear and cohomomorphism:

e_{\alpha,ij}^{*}=e_{\alpha,ji}.

(S49)

The unit, counit, and antipode are defined as

\begin{split}&{\bf 1}=\sum_{\alpha,i}e_{\alpha,ii},\\
&\epsilon(e_{I,ij})=1,\\
&\epsilon(e_{\tau,ij})=0,\\
&S(e_{I,ij})=e_{I,ji},\\
&S(e_{\tau,ij})=\zeta^{\xi(i)-\xi(j)}e_{\tau,\pi(j)\pi(i)},\end{split}

(S50)

where
\pi,\xi
are permutations of the set
\{1,2,3\}
with
\pi
swaps
1\leftrightarrow 2
and
\xi
swaps
2\leftrightarrow 3
.

There exists a pairing

\langle,\rangle:\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}\otimes\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C},

(S51)

which is a non-degenerate bilinear form that satisfies
[
62
]
:

\begin{split}&\langle x\cdot x^{\prime},y\rangle=\langle x\otimes x^{\prime},\Delta(y)\rangle,\quad\quad\langle{\bf 1},y\rangle=\epsilon(y),\\
&\langle x,y\cdot y^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\Delta(x),y\otimes y^{\prime}\rangle,\quad\quad\langle x,{\bf 1}\rangle=\epsilon(x),\\
&\langle S(x),y\rangle=\langle x,S(y)\rangle,\quad\text{and}\quad\langle x^{*},y\rangle=\overline{\langle x,[S(y)]^{*}\rangle},\end{split}

(S52)

where the overline denotes the complex conjugation. The pairing induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}\cong\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}^{*}
. In terms of basis, let us denote

\langle e_{\alpha^{\prime},ij},e_{\alpha,kl}\rangle=\tilde{R}_{\alpha,kl}^{\alpha^{\prime},ij},

(S53)

and the isomorphism maps
e_{\alpha,ij}
to
\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},kl}\tilde{R}_{\alpha^{\prime},kl}^{\alpha,ij}\tilde{e}_{\alpha^{\prime},kl}\in\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}^{*}
. We have a faithful *-representation of
\mathcal{A}
:
\Phi(e_{\alpha,ij})=|\alpha,ij\rangle\langle\alpha,ij|
. From the pairing-induced isomorphism, we can construct a faithful *-representation
\Psi
for
\mathcal{A}_{\text{Fib}}^{*}
satisfying

\Psi(\sum_{\alpha,kl}\tilde{R}_{a,kl}^{\alpha,ij}\tilde{e}_{a,kl})=|a,i)(a,j|,

(S54)

where we used notations introduced in the main text. Viewing
\tilde{R}_{a,kl}^{\alpha,ij}
as a matrix of combined indices
\{\alpha,i,j\}
and
\{a,k,l\}
, the
R
matrix in the main text is the inverse of
\tilde{R}
and

\Psi(\tilde{e}_{\alpha,ij})=\sum_{a,kl}R_{a,kl}^{\alpha,ij}|a,k)(a,l|.

(S55)

The construction for
\tilde{R}_{a,kl}^{\alpha,ij}
is:

\begin{split}\tilde{R}_{I,11}^{I,11}&=\tilde{R}_{I,12}^{\tau,11}=\tilde{R}_{\tau,11}^{I,12}=\tilde{R}_{I,21}^{\tau,22}=\tilde{R}_{\tau,22}^{I,21}\\
&=\tilde{R}_{\tau,21}^{\tau,21}=\tilde{R}_{\tau,23}^{\tau,31}=\tilde{R}_{\tau,31}^{\tau,23}=1,\\
\tilde{R}_{\tau,32}^{\tau,32}&=\tilde{R}_{\tau,13}^{\tau,13}=\zeta^{-1},\quad\tilde{R}_{\tau,12}^{\tau,12}=\zeta^{-2},\\
\tilde{R}_{\tau,33}^{I,22}&=\tilde{R}_{I,22}^{\tau,33}=-\tilde{R}_{\tau,33}^{\tau,33}=\zeta^{2},\quad\tilde{R}_{I,22}^{I,22}=\zeta^{4},\\
\end{split}

(S56)

and any other unspecified components are zero. The
R
matrix as its inverse has its nonzero components:

\begin{split}R_{I,11}^{I,11}&=R_{I,12}^{\tau,11}=R_{\tau,11}^{I,12}=R_{I,21}^{\tau,22}=R_{\tau,22}^{I,21}=R_{I,22}^{I,22}\\
&=R_{I,22}^{\tau,33}=R_{\tau,33}^{I,22}=R_{\tau,21}^{\tau,21}=R_{\tau,23}^{\tau,31}=R_{\tau,31}^{\tau,23}=1,\\
R_{\tau,13}^{\tau,13}&=R_{\tau,32}^{\tau,32}=\zeta,\quad R_{\tau,12}^{\tau,12}=-R_{\tau,33}^{\tau,33}=\zeta^{2}.\end{split}

(S57)

From the constructions of
\Phi
and
\Psi
, one can obtain the MPO tensor
\sum_{\alpha,ij}\Phi(e_{\alpha,ij})\otimes\Psi(\tilde{e}_{\alpha,ij})
. The result can be alternatively derived from the
F
-symbols of Fibonacci fusion category, see e.g.,
[
39
]
. Our derivation naturally ensures
\Psi
to be a faithful *-representation of
\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\text{Fib}}
.

In our construction, there are two irreps
I
and
\tau
, whose quantum dimensions are
d_{I}=1
and
d_{\tau}=(\sqrt{5}+1)/2
, and the projections to them is already trivially encoded in our construction. Let us now obtain the
O^{(L)}(\Pi_{m})
as MPO representations of the central idempotents corresponding to 1D irreps of Fibonacci fusion algebra. The nonzero components of fusion matrices are
N_{II}^{I}=N_{I\tau}^{\tau}=N_{\tau I}^{\tau}=N_{\tau\tau}^{I}=N_{\tau\tau}^{\tau}=1
. From the regular representation, we obtain two 1D irreps of the fusion algebra
\mathcal{R}
and there are two corresponding central idempotents which should sum to the unit of the algebra. In MPO representions we have:

O^{(L)}(\Pi_{0})+O^{(L)}(\Pi_{1})=O^{(L)}_{I}.

(S58)

Since we know

O^{(L)}(\Pi_{0})=\frac{1}{\mathcal{D}^{2}}(d_{I}O_{I}^{(L)}+d_{\tau}O_{\tau}^{(L)})=\frac{2}{5+\sqrt{5}}O_{I}^{(L)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}O_{\tau}^{(L)},

(S59)

we must have

O^{(L)}(\Pi_{1})=\frac{2}{5-\sqrt{5}}O_{I}^{(L)}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}O_{\tau}^{(L)}.

(S60)

These equations give the result of Eq. (20) in the main text. The corresponding symmetry eigenvalues are
\lambda_{0I}=1
,
\lambda_{0\tau}=\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}
, and
\lambda_{1I}=1
,
\lambda_{1\tau}=\frac{-\sqrt{5}+1}{2}
.
